#2180. Selfie-seekers bring Man Utd vs Leeds match to premature end

Aren’t friendlies supposed to be non-events? Manchester United against Leeds United in Dublin always had the capacity to be lively, but try explaining the half hour that Bruno Fernandes experienced in one breath. It would be impossible.

There was only one player the vast majority of the crowd wanted to see in the grandiosely named Boyle Sports Cup and, in chronological order from his 60th minute introduction, he received a standing ovation, hit the bar, had a goal disallowed, missed a penalty in a shoot-out and lifted a trophy.

Most remarkably – and, certainly, worryingly – of all, he was central to the game being ended early as five young boys, one after the other, breached security and ran to him in the middle of pitch, all of them successfully posing for photos with the Portuguese.

You could say it was harmless fun but it should not have happened and the flummoxed faces that were pulled on the Manchester United bench, as an army in high-visibility jackets stood and watched it all unfold, suggested they took a dim view.

It was a shame because in that cameo, Fernandes showed he has returned from the World Cup in excellent shape, the tempo of United’s play immediately lifting when he had the ball. It is wrong to make conclusions in the summer, but you are on safe ground to state he will be integral to their ambitions.

Croke Park is a stadium steeped in history and another chapter was penned here, as the capacity crowd made this the highest attended soccer match in Ireland with 82,000 coming through the gates: it is a magnificent venue, one that would be well capable of hosting a Champions League final.

Michael Carrick hopes in years to come that is the stage where his team will be but, right now, it is a case of learning to walk again before they can run and he more than anyone will know how far off that target is, not that it will stop him trying to get there.

The one thing that is obvious about the way United play under him is the first thought from everyone is to go forward quickly. Yes, this was only a friendly, but the ambition to be on the front foot and cause problems was evident from the start.

The team in red had much the better of the opening exchanges, with Diogo Dalot having the first shot that went over but, unlike on July 25, when Mayo beat Kerry here in Gaelic football’s All Ireland final, which went viral, nobody was cheering the sight of a ball clearing the bar.

It was like a home game for United and the majority got what they wanted in the 15th minute when a slick counter-attack ended with Bryan Mbuemo sliding a pass into Joshua Zikzee, who rifled past James Trafford with a first-time finish.

Devastating counterattack! 💥Manchester United break and Joshua Zirkzee is there to finish it off 🤩 pic.twitter.com/y6QHbZMi7n

Zirkzee is unlikely to be part of Carrick’s squad beyond the transfer window, with interest from clubs in Italy, and it would be disingenuous to suggest his career at Old Trafford has been successful, but his attitude was exemplary and he played as if he was desperate to change his manager’s mind.

There may have been an anticipation that United would stride clear, but this game was also about Leeds, whose summer has been encouraging and whose confidence from staying up last season has evidently enabled them to blossom.

You would be way off the mark if you include them in your conversations about which clubs are going to be relegated next May, and the sense they showed to stay with Daniel Farke has paid off. They have bought some good players, not least goalkeeper James Trafford and Harry Wilson.

Top stop from James Trafford! 🚫The Leeds United keeper has been busy in the first half... pic.twitter.com/VKmxNVKBsA

Trafford has done the right thing getting away from Manchester City and you could argue that he is already reaping the benefit of playing for a manager who wants him, as he made a fabulous save in the 23rd minute to thwart Mbuemo. It was all part of performance that led to him being named player of the match.

He was involved in the move that brought the Leeds equaliser, too, as his long clearance caused uncertainty and led to Tyler Fletcher being pickpocketed by Dan James and he nudged a pass into Brenden Aaronson, who kept his cool and danced into the area, ignoring Senne Lammens, to finish.

Leeds hit back with their first shot on target! 💥Brenden Aaronson with a cool finish 🥶 pic.twitter.com/5WsSZY41j6

It was an entertaining affair, mercifully lacking the bile that would usually bubble away when it is staged in England, and the longer the game advanced, the better Leeds looked. They finished the opening period strongly and came back after the restart, with 10 changes, in the same fettle.

Farke, whose team had shown their ability to score heavily when they pummelled Liverpool 4-2 in Chicago 10 days ago, would have taken plenty of encouragement, but the perfectionist in him would have cursed the opportunities that were squandered early in the second period.

The misses proved costly as the game meandered and the shoot-out went United’s way, after Wilson and James Justin failed from 12 yards, with Noussair Mazraoui thumping in the decisive kick. It really is never dull.

Manchester United Lammens (Mee 61); Dalot (Mazraoui 60), Yoro, Heaven (Armer 46), Amass (Shaw 60); Santos (Collyer 46), T Fletcher (Tielemans 61); Diallo (J Fletcher 60), Zirkzee (Cunha 60), Dorgu (Thompson 46); Mbuemo (Fernandes 60).Subs not used Heath, Honor, Lacey, Gabriel.

Decent enough watch for roughly half the game, settled on a shoot-out in which Alex Cairns got nowhere near any of Manchester United’s penalties. Doubt he will lose much sleep over it. Bruno Fernandes grinned broadly when he missed.

Just the 10 half-time changes for Leeds and the worst thing about that was the fact it gave the stadium announcer, who hadn’t grasped that he didn’t need to shout into a loud speaker system, the opportunity to screech unchecked for close to two minutes, leaving everyone with tinnitus. Give the wrong man a microphone and chaos will ensue. Anyway, we are underway in the second period and the one change the majority of the stadium wants to be see is the introduction of Bruno Fernandes. His presence would help United regain some impetus.

Trafford confidently catches the cross from the corner then bowls it out 40 yards to set Aaronson off on a counter. His solo raid down the right is stymied by Amass’s diligent defending.

Mbeumo drives down the gap between Muharemovic and Bijol, tries to find Doegu but Bornauw slides in to intercept but only succeeds in playing it back to Mbeumo who lashes a left-foot shot that Trafford tips round the right post at full stretch.

Top stop from James Trafford! 🚫The Leeds United keeper has been busy in the first half... pic.twitter.com/VKmxNVKBsA

There is certainly more urgency about Manchester United’s play. Leeds are living dangerously.

In the couple of minutes after the goal Manchester United almost punish Leeds’ high defensive line again but Muharemovic rides to the rescue. Bijol is a very good box defender but he lacks pace.

United’s start has been bright and confident and the system they playing is flipping between 4-4-2 and 4-2-3-1 and the first thought is always to go forward. The goal they have just scored is indicative of how they have started and it’s a positive for Joshua Zirkzee, who has spent much of his time at Old Trafford under a cloud, to have finished it with aplomb. Whether it will be enough to keep him at Old Trafford for the long run remains to be seen, particularly as interest from Italy persists, but what can’t be doubted is his attitude. He looks sharp.

Manchester United 1 Leeds United 0 (Zirkzee) Leeds, on the attack, lose possession down the middle and Dorgu works it out to Mbeumo who beats Bijol 20 yards inside the Man Utd half. He pokes it through the Leeds captain;s legs and hares off down the wing until he gets to the 18 yard line and from there he bends the perfect pass to Zirkzee who sweeps home a shot into the bottom left with his left foot.

Devastating counterattack! 💥Manchester United break and Joshua Zirkzee is there to finish it off 🤩 pic.twitter.com/y6QHbZMi7n

Manchester United build slowly down the right and then Dalot injects some urgency by whipping in a low cross. Zirkzee meets it not so much with a shot but with the timing of his arrival on the ball, deflecting it towards the right of goal powerfully, Trafford gets down well to save.

Leeds cross the free-kick into the box and James’ volley strikes a defender in the midriff and then hits the deck when going for the rebound, bundled over by Yoro. The ref waves play on and Manchester Unted counter with Amad Diallo dribbling down the right until tackled by Chadwick at the price of a corner.

Dorgu is penalised for a scissors-tackle on Bornauw. He did get the ball but only after he had caught he blond defender with his other leg. Carrick leaps up to protest, as does Farke but he is saying that Dorgu has twice slid in wildly.

Leeds defend the corner fairly solidly but Manchester United recover possession 30 yards out and set up Dalot to have a crack with a dipping, swerving effort that Trafford pats down in front of his face and catches after it bounces.

Amad Diallo finds Mbeumo down the right and he tries to head the ball across the box but Muharemovic is well-placed to head it back out but an attempt to nod the ball back to Trafford goes astray and the £45m man can’t stop it going out for a corner.

Leeds free-kick for either a foul by Amass on Aaronson or Dorgu on Bogle. Hard to tell, one was a split-second after the other.

Amass pulls back Bogle after being tackled by the Leeds wing-back and Leeds build up the right from the free-kick, earning themselves a throw-in parallel with the penalty spot. In Ampadu’s absence Tanaka is the trebuchet, hurling in the long throw that Bornauw nods on but Manchester united clear the second ball.

Leeds work the ball back to Taafford from the kick off and he chips it long up the right. Dorgu heads it out for a throw-in. Leeds go backwards again and pass the ball around confidently.

Jaka Bijol captains Leeds, Diogo Dalot leads out Manchester United.

They would be as well delaying the start of the game here the concourses outside this magnificent and historic stadium are packed with crowds taking in the solar eclipse. What a genuinely extraordinary moment! There will be plenty of time later this evening to talk about the football, for now attentions are firmly elsewhere!

🤩 Ready for a first #LUFC start! pic.twitter.com/KU5ica0HcG

And for Senne Lammens, a new shirt number, from 31 to Numero Uno:

Lammens is not the only one. Marcus Rashford wore 39 in his debut season with Man Utd, moved to 19 for the next three then changed to 10 when Zlatan Ibrahimovic left the club. The 10 shirt has been given to Matheus Cunha so now he will wear 14, the number he had at Barcelona in his Liga title-winning season.

If you’re watching in the UK, the match is live on ITV. A friendly! Live on ITV.  That’s Croke Park for you.

Good evening and welcome to live coverage of the pre-season friendly between Manchester United and Leeds United at a sold-out Croke Park in Dublin. Last year Leeds played Milan in the Irish capital and drew a crowd of 52,000 but at the GAA citadel, as I think I’m contractually obliged to call it, there will be 83,000 present, a testament to the Premier League’s reach and these two clubs’ popularity in Ireland founded on the exploits of Billy Whelan, Noel Cantwell, Johnny Giles, Roy Keane, Gary Kelly et al. It’s the first club match ever held at the stadium.

It’s a fixture that always stirs the blood and whenever it comes around you can guarantee that there will be reams of stuff about the War of the Roses, clash in styles, Busby and Revie, Bobby and Jack, large swathes of which this author has been guilty of producing. I’ll spare you all that today because while it’s relevant to fans of both clubs, they know it backwards. And it’s a match that has come around a wee bit too frequently in pre-season recently, first in Perth, Western Australia, in 2019 when Ole Gunnar Solskjaer’s side beat Marcelo Bielsa’s 4-0, then in 2023 in Oslo when Erik ten Haag’s second or third string defeated Daniel Farke’s threadbare, just relegated squad 2-0 and last year in Stockholm, which ended in a 0-0 draw between two scratch sides.

This one comes round later in the pre-season schedule than those three so we can expect to see stronger XIs, at least for part of the game. Leeds’ pre-season has gone well: after a 3-2 defeat by Wrexham in Tampa, they have beaten Sunderland in New Jersey, Liverpool in Chicago and RB Leipzig at Elland Road. Manchester United also lost to Wrexham (in Helsinki) then beat Rosenborg in Trondheim, Atlético in Stockholm and drew one apiece with the European champions, PSG, in Gothenburg last time out.

While Leeds will have new signing James Trafford available and Noah Okafor, who scored twice in Leeds’ first league victory at Old Trafford for 45 years last April, in the squad for the first time after the World Cup, Manchester United have Kobbie Mainoo and Marcus Rashford back in training since the start of the week but it seems too soon for them to start but Youri Tielemans is pushing to make his first start.

Lucas Perri, portiere del Torino, ha parlato in Press Conference dopo la sconfitta contro il Napoli: “Abbiamo giocato molto bene. Dispiace per la sconfitta. Torniamo a casa senza punti. Dobbiamo continuare a lavorare, e a combattere in ogni partita. L’esordio col Brasile? Mi ha fatto molto piacere, e’ stato un sogno realizzato, un momento speciale per me e per la mia famiglia. #2177. Sheffield United need to play 22-year-old next season when he returns to Bramall Lane: Opinion

Sheffield United let the attacker head out the exit door on a temporary basis again in the summer to help him get some more experience under his belt. He has since flourished away from SUFC and they will be pleased with his development in League Two.

Marsh, 22, has enjoyed plenty of game time with the Spirerites under the guidance of their head coach Paul Cook and he has become one of their key players over recent times. He has made 16 appearances in all competitions in this campaign, 12 of which have come in the league, and he has chipped in with an eye-catching eight goals and three assists to date.

He is due to return to the Blades next year and he remains under contract with the South Yorkshire outfit until June 2028 so still just under two years left to run down. Chris Wilder should have him in his long-term plans after his impressive campaign to date.

Sheffield United are aiming to get into the Championship play-offs this term. They are sat in 10th place and are two points off the top eight behind both QPR and Southampton after 15 fixtures.

Sheffield United will see Marsh as one for the future. He is versatile and can play as a centre-forward or as an attacking midfielder which means he will be a useful weapon for SUFC down the line.

At the age of 22, he has bags of potential and the capacity to improve over the coming years. The Blades will be keeping tabs on his development at Chesterfield and will be satisfied with how he is doing.

The ex-England youth international, who played for the Three Lions’ U20s side back in 2024, has risen up through the academy at Bramall Lane. He was a regular at various different youth levels.

Marsh has played five times for Sheffield United’s senior team to date. Prior to his switch to Chesterfield, he was loaned out to Doncaster Rovers, Fleetwood Town and Falkirk to get some exposure to men’s football.

Sheffield United have an option to extend Patrick Bamford‘s stay until 2028. However, there are question marks over the long-term futures of Tyrese Campbell, Tom Cannon and Jordan Ayes, whilst Romelle Donovan and Ben Brereton Diaz will return to Brentford and Southampton respectively next year.

Therefore, the Blades will be short of attacking options by next term. That means that Marsh has an ideal opportunity to stake a claim when he is back from Chesterfield.

#2175. Serie B, Avellino-Mantova: match tra due squadre che sognano l'alta classifica

Per Avellino e Mantova è tempo di tornare in campo. Al Partenio, dunque, andrà di scena una gara tra due squadre che, anche grazie al ko del Palermo contro il Catanzaro, potrebbero fare un grosso salto in classifica. Ci si aspetta una grande partita da questi due team. L’arbitro dell’incontro sarà Matteo Drigo della sezione di Portogruaro, mentre al VAR ci sarà Dionisi.

COME ARRIVA L’AVELLINO - L’ultima gara degli uomini di Nesta prima della sosta è l’1-1 nel derby contro il Benevento. Lo score dell’Avellino è comunque positivo, se si considerano il pari di Chiavari contro l’Entella e, soprattutto, la vittoria in casa contro il Pisa. Alessandro Nesta, nella consueta conferenza stampa della vigilia, ha introdotto così il match: “La squadra ha lavorato bene, anche se non manca qualche problemino. Il Mantova è difficile da affrontare e dobbiamo essere consapevoli della gara che ci aspetta. Occorre un salto di qualità mentale”. Ecco la probabile formazione irpina, che dovrebbe giocare con un 3-5-2: Martinelli; Izzo, Venturi, Enrici; Missori, Palumbo, Palmiero, Besaggio, Moruzzi; Russo, Cheddira.

COME ARRIVA IL MANTOVA - Così come l’Avellino, di fatto, anche il Mantova ha pareggiato nell’ultimo turno di campionato prima della sosta. I biancorossi hanno pareggiato in casa 0-0 contro il Verona. Gli uomini di Modesto, comunque consapevoli delle difficoltà della gara, voglio tornare al successo in trasferta, cosa che manca dal successo contro l’Entella. Lo stesso Modesto ha ribadito in conferenza stampa cosa si aspetta dalla sfida del Partenio: “I ragazzi hanno portato avanti un bel programma e confido che riusciremo a fornire una prestazione di livello. Sarà una gara molto tosta, ma sono convinto che la squadra si farà trovare pronta”. Ecco la probabile formazione del Mantova (3-4-2-1): Bardi; Tomasevic, Cella, Castellini; Radaelli, Trimboli, Kouda, Benaissa; Ilie, Ruocco; Gliozzi.

#2174. Cardiff City will surely sell 2022 signing in January, contract up next year: Opinion

Cardiff City have left the attacker out of their last five squads, despite him being fit and available to play. They are positioned in 14th place in the Championship table after their first 14 fixtures of the season.

Robinson, 31, has made seven appearances for the Bluebirds in all competitions in this campaign, six of which have come in the league, and he has chipped in with two goals. However, he hasn’t played for the Welsh side since their home match against Watford in mid-October.

The Republic of Ireland international, who has 38 caps to his name, is now facing an uncertain long-term future at the Cardiff City Stadium ahead of the January transfer window and his current club will surely look to sell him this winter. He is out of contract at the end of this term and is due to become a free agent in June 2027.

Cardiff City beat Burnley 5-4 in a crazy game last time out before the international break. They will be in confident mood now as they gear up for their upcoming away trip to Norwich City this weekend.

Cardiff City risk losing Robinson for nothing when this season ends and he is unlikely to be offered an extension amid his lack of minutes. Therefore, most signs point towards a sale in the near future to ensure that they get a bit of cash for him.

The Northampton-born man has become surplus to requirements with the Bluebirds. He has found it hard to get into Brian Barry-Murphy’s plans ahead of Jack Moylan and Yousef Salach.

Robinson, who rose up through Aston Villa’s academy in his early career, has been with Cardiff City since joining them in 2022. He has since been a good servant and has played 137 games.

Prior to his switch to South Wales, he had a spell at West Brom. He has also featured for the likes of Preston North End and Sheffield United in the past and is experienced in the EFL.

Robinson would benefit from a change of scene away from Cardiff City. Meanwhile, the Bluebirds would free up space and funds to pave the way for other reinforcements.

He played a key role behind their promotion from League One and found the net on eight occasions. However, he is out of favour now though and a departure seems inevitable.

#2173. Raul Jimenez’s late penalty seals share of spoils for Wolves against Blackburn

An injury-time penalty from returning hero Raul Jimenez ensured Wolves came away with a point in their Championship opener after Blackburn had produced a comeback.

Wolves led through Marshall Munetsi’s cool finish on 18 minutes only for Sean McLoughlin’s first Blackburn goal to draw the visitors level on 27 minutes with a well-timed run and header.

The visitors turned the game around on 48 minutes through Andri Gudjohnsen’s close-range strike only for substitute Jimenez to level the game three minutes into injury time from the spot after Ryan Alebiosu handled the ball in the box. Jimenez and his wife marked his return to Molineux by giving 5,000 fans black scarves emblazoned with the words ‘Si Senor’ - a nod to his song - and Jimenez did not disappoint as he rifled in his penalty.

LATE DRAMA! 😱Raul Jimenez cooly converts from the penalty spot, deep into added time to save a point for Wolves 🔥 pic.twitter.com/5h2lMxcHNU

Cesar Peixoto wants Wolves to play with their heads rather than their hearts after the home side rescued a late point in the first game of the Championship season following relegation. Wolves replaced Rob Edwards with Portuguese coach Peixoto during the summer and he felt his side’s loss of organisation allowed Blackburn to take control of the game.

Peixoto said: “One point is not what we want, we want three points. We lost some organisation in the second half and played more with our hearts and not our heads and brains.

“We have to keep our mental stability during the 90 minutes because we’re better when we’re organised. We need to play our way with our identity. It’s a lesson for us to understand when we lose organisation it’s more difficult for us and more easy for the opposition.

“We’re working hard to bring more solutions [into the club] but we need to work hard with the players we have. It’s the first game, there’s a lot to work on and a lot to improve.”

Tony Mowbray took charge of Blackburn in the league for the first time since returning to the club for a second stint in the dugout. Rovers recorded their lowest league finish since Mowbray’s first stint in charge of the club last term but the Blackburn boss is confident his side have enough to beat the drop and climb up the table this campaign.

Mowbray said: “Ultimately, it feels like a defeat, it’s a bit disappointing and a bit frustrating. I just felt a lot of decisions were starting to swing their way. It’s harsh to take, I don’t think they deserved a second goal. We asked the players to be brave and we were collectively very strong.

“Everybody has us relegated, probably on the back of finishing 20th last season and losing a couple of our better players and not spending much money, which is a good motivation for the team. We deserved to win the game, we showed there’s enough quality to give teams problems and I hope there’s help on the way [in the transfer window].”

The first chance of the season came the way of Blackburn as a cross-field pass found Alebiosu down the right, who floated a ball into the box only for an unmarked Oladipo Afolayan to head wide from eight-yards out.

However, Wolves scored the first goal of the new campaign when Fer Lopez played a defence-splitting through ball into the path of Munetsi, who coolly found the net with a first time effort from the edge of the penalty area.

"A vibrant Molineux rejoices!" 🎉Marshall Munetsi cooly slots home for Wolves to give them the lead on the opening night of the Championship season 🥶 pic.twitter.com/tVHdbNv3Hq

Blackburn drew level through an unlikely source just nine minutes later as McLoughlin headed in his first-ever goal in English football in more than 150 games when he met Ryoya Morishita’s cross from the right at the near post.

The home side had chances to retake the lead before the break as Lopez curled a shot narrowly over the top from 20-yards while Kieran Trippier teed up Mateus Mane for a chance as the teenager found the top of the net from distance.

Blackburn turned the game on its head minutes after the break as Gudjohnsen turned home Afolayan’s deflected ball in from the right from close range to stun Molineux into silence.

"It's brilliantly worked by Blackburn Rovers!" 💥Andri Guðjohnsen finishes from close range to turn the game around and the visitors the lead 👏 pic.twitter.com/vkKQQezicK

Peixoto introduced Jimenez from the bench on the hour mark but even the Mexican could not lift Wolves with Blackburn comfortable for long periods as frustration grew in the stands.

However, Alebiosu handled Jackson Tchatchoua’s cross from the left in injury time and the referee awarded a spot kick which allowed Jimenez to mark his Wolves return with an equaliser from 12-yards as he sent Balazs Toth the wrong way.

Wolverhampton: Bentley, Trippier, Mosquera, Toti Gomes, Wolfe, Lopez, Andre Trindade, Munetsi, Rodrigo Gomes, Armstrong, Mane.Subs used: Jiménez 60 (Mateus Mané), ‌Doyle ‌67 (Rodrigo Gomes), ⁠Tchatchoua 67 (Trippier), Pedro Lima 79 (André),Chirewa 79 (Møller Wolfe)Scorers: M. McLoughlin 27, A. Gudjohnsen 48Yellow card: Pickering ​34, Cantwell 35, ‌Forshaw 45Attendance: 30,984Referee: Farai ⁠Hallam

“I think it is a really harsh one. He [the referee] has to be sure at such a critical time of the game. And I do not think he can be sure. I think he has almost had a guess that he is in the box. It is such a big call.”

“We see the ref’s position. The fact the ref makes the decision to give the penalty in my opinion, it is a bigger decision to give it then to not. We have got the luxury of the replays and we are still not sure. His whole body is outside the box.”

“It looks outside the box to me [the penalty incident]. The still picture suggests to me it was outside the box. We can get better and we will have to. We have to be more dominant with the ball at times and be better in attack. We have done a lot of work on that in pre-season. Having been in this league a long time, the power of the dressing room is crucial and any motivation tool you can you use is important. I think we will be fine. You have to enjoy football and love it. I think the boys enjoyed it tonight.”

“Obviously frustrated. The boys did everything to get the three points but it is gutting in the end. I thought I was outside the box when the ball came in and the decision went against us. They are saying it is in the box and it is frustrating. We dug deep and thought we had it. I cannot fault everyone as we all put in a shift, it was just the final details with the penalty decision. We deserved the three points. If we play like that every week, we will go far in the table.”

Raul Jimenez reacts after his late penalty saved a point for Wolves against Blackburn 🗣️ pic.twitter.com/aXvVeWw6fc

“Before the game we were predicting Wolves would be right up there [this season] and we thought it would be a comfortable win. But that was not the case.  Blackburn gave a really good account of themselves and had lots of openings. [Cesar] Peixoto will be breathing a massive sigh of relief with that handball.”

“Wolves got away with one if I am being really honest. In terms of their second-half performance, they did not do anywhere near enough. I thought Blackburn were excellent throughout and deservedly took the lead. It is a huge decision [to give the penalty], but in the end Raul Jimenez steps up to get his side the point. [Cesar Peixoto] will be delighted to get something in the end.”

When Wolverhampton Wanderers last attacked the Championship under a Portuguese manager new to English football, it proved a vintage season.

Molineux was under the spell of Nuno Espírito Santo in that 2017-18 campaign, and the big question is whether César Peixoto can lead a repeat nearly a decade later.

Peixoto was a surprise appointment in June, replacing Rob Edwards, and he has been entrusted with the mission of returning Wolves to the Premier League at the first time of asking.

“I have changed some things with set-pieces and some things in other moments of the game because I want the team to be organised and understand all the different moments within a match. During pre-season we have scored some goals from high pressing, but we will not be able to press high in every moment of every game. The players have to understand the game and recognise what each moment requires. It is important that we work as a team. When we can press high, we’ll press high. When we need to defend in a mid-block, we will stay organised. When the opposition pushes us deeper, which will sometimes happen, we have to be organised there as well.

“There are a lot of crosses in the Championship, for example, so the team needs to understand what to do in those situations. We want to play our football, be offensive and react strongly when we lose the ball, but we also need to understand the different moments of the game. I think we are at a good level to attack the Championship.”

Our experts have been having their say ahead of the new Championship season. A number of them believe Wolves will be promoted, including John Percy:

It is difficult to look beyond West Ham. Nuno Espírito Santo romped to the Championship title with Wolves in 2018 and remains a shrewd operator (along with his No 2 Rui Pedro Silva), while convincing Jarrod Bowen to stay is significant. I am going to go for Wolves and Middlesbrough as the other two promoted clubs.

🔢 Team news...2⃣ changes✅ Kristi Montgomery & Andri Gudjohnsen come in🆕 Sam Morsy on the bench🤕 Augustus Kargbo, Dion De Neve, Scott Wharton, Moussa Baradji, Mathias Jorgensen & Lewis Miller on the injured list#WOLvROV | #Rovers 🔵⚪️ pic.twitter.com/esyE5Fibqc

The 2026/27 Championship season kicks off at Molineux as Wolves host Blackburn, with the home side hoping to put the bad memories of last season firmly behind them. There are new playoff rules in place for this season, with six places now up for grabs in the play-offs.

Wolves are aiming to return to the Premier League at the first attempt, having endured a miserable season in the top flight last year as they finished bottom with just three wins to their name. It has been a summer of change at the club as they chose to change managers. Rob Edwards lost his job in what was a shock move and was replaced by 46-year-old Cesar Peixoto, with this being his first head coaching position outside of his home country Portugal. Back in 2017/18, Wolves won the Championship title led by Peixoto’s compatriot Nuno Espirito Santo.

“I am ready,” Peixoto told Wolves’ club website. “We have worked very hard during pre-season, and I think we have had a great pre-season. All the players have worked very, very hard and they understand what I want from them. I am ready and confident going into the first game. Football is football, whether it is in Portugal or England, but there are different characteristics to the game here. We have watched, observed and analysed the Championship. We did that work before I came to the club, so we know what to expect.

“It is going to be a tough season with good teams, but we are prepared to fight for the three points game after game and week after week. We know what we want from the team and we know what our big goal is. We’re going to work very hard and fight to achieve that goal.”

They have brought in vast experience on the player side in the shape of Kieran Trippier and Raul Jimenez, who is back at Molineux three years after leaving for Fulham. Although they have lost Brazilian midfielder Joao Gomes to Aston Villa, most of their squad has stayed intact as they pursue promotion back to the top flight. It is a special year for the club as they celebrate their 150th anniversary.

Blackburn, led by experienced manager Tony Mowbray, did not enjoy a great season in the Championship last year, finishing down in 20th place and just five points above the relegation zone. 14 years have now passed since Blackburn last played in the top flight.

“I think it is a tough start,” Mowbray said. “On paper, I would suggest the first couple of games, particularly, jumped out as a tough start. One of the Premier League sides from last season, away from home. They have got a very talented squad, a pretty expensive squad. They have got some very good individual talent. Yet, there is an argument to say they have not won many football matches in the last calendar year. So, what we get, we will have to wait and see.”

These sides last met in the FA Cup during the 2024/25 season, with Wolves winning that fourth-round tie 2-0 thanks to goals from Matheus Cunha and Gomes, both of whom are now plying their trade elsewhere. The omens would appear to be on Wolves’ side as they are unbeaten in their last eight league meetings with Blackburn. Both sides progressed to the second round of the League Cup last weekend, with Wolves beating Port Vale 3-0 and Blackburn overcoming Burton Albion 2-1.

#2172. Serie B, Arezzo-Padova: Bucchi cerca la scossa, veneti per continuare la serie positiva

Appuntamento questo pomeriggio alle 17.15 allo stadio "Città di Arezzo" per la sfida tra Arezzo e Padova, incrocio di Serie BKT che mette in palio punti pesantissimi tra zona calda e parte sinistra della classifica. Da una parte il bisogno urgente di muovere la classifica, dall'altra la voglia di allungare il momento positivo: gli amaranto di Cristian Bucchi occupano la 16° posizione con 11 punti (a +2 dall'ultimo posto) e pagano un bilancio recente di una sola vittoria in sette partite; i biancoscudati si presentano invece al 12° posto a quota 15, ad un solo punto dal treno playoff e imbattuti da quattro turni.

COME ARRIVA L'AREZZO - Il momento in casa amaranto resta delicato: con appena 9 reti all'attivo, il Cavallino ha fin qui il peggior attacco del torneo e fatica a trovare soluzioni concrete negli ultimi metri, cercando ancora la prima gioia interna. L’obiettivo resta la salvezza, da inseguire passando necessariamente attraverso una scossa offensiva e ad un cambio di passo davanti al proprio pubblico per dare continuità al lavoro settimanale. Nonostante le difficoltà e una classifica corta che non concede distrazioni, la dirigenza toscana ha blindato mister Bucchi, confermando piena fiducia nel tecnico e nella capacità del gruppo di compattarsi. Sul fronte infermeria restano indisponibili Viviani, Oliveri, Chierico e Ferrari, costringendo lo staff a ridisegnare le rotazioni.

COME ARRIVA IL PADOVA - I patavini attraversano una fase brillante, forte di quattro risultati utili consecutivi che hanno portato i biancoscudati a 15 punti, ad una sola lunghezza dalla zona playoff e con l'inerzia giusta per affrontare una trasferta insidiosa con fiducia. L’obiettivo del club resta il consolidamento in Serie B attraverso una salvezza tranquilla, da costruire mantenendo ritmo, compattezza e solidità nella parte sinistra della graduatoria senza perdere di vista l'equilibrio tra le fasi. Sul fronte rosa, arrivano segnali incoraggianti dai rientri nei ranghi dell'esterno offensivo Zanimacchia e di Barreca, due opzioni preziose per allargare le rotazioni e dare maggiore spinta sulle corsie. Resta invece lunga e impegnativa la lista degli indisponibili, che costringe lo staff a fare i conti con diverse assenze di rilievo: out Lovato, Sgarbi, Dellavalle, Galazzi, Caprari, Lasagna e Buonaiuto.

#2171. West Brom have to consider deal to re-sign Ipswich Town man in January transfer window: Opinion

West Brom will get the chance to bring in some new faces this winter ahead of the second-half of the season. WBA are currently in the Championship play-offs along with Stoke City, Wolves, Swansea City, Southampton and QPR after 15 fixtures.

The Baggies were dealt a blow at the start of last month when Noah Galves was ruled out for the rest of the campaign with a serious knee injury. That has left them short of options at full-back and they would benefit from another option to battle with Brayann Pereira.

Step forward Ipswich Town defender Darnell Furlong, who could be made available due to a lack of game time at Portman Road following the Tractor Boys’ promotion to the Premier League. He was on the books at The Hawthorns from 2019 to 2025 and made 247 appearances in all competitions for the Midlands outfit, chipping in with 11 goals from the back.

West Brom could get the opportunity now to try and lure him back in some capacity. They are back in action this weekend with an away trip to Swansea City.

Furlong is under contract at Ipswich Town until June 2028. However, he has slipped down the pecking order in Suffolk and has made only three outings so far this term, one one of which has come in the league.

A change of scene to West Brom would make sense for him. He would be able to get some regular game time under his belt at WBA and help their push for promotion under James Morrison.

Furlong, who is from Luton, knows the club inside out already and is a solid operator in the EFL. He knows what it takes to get out of the second tier and would be a useful character to have in and around the dressing room.

Prior to his switch to the Baggies, he rose up the ranks at QPR and played 72 times for their first-team in his early career. He also had spells away from the Hoops at Northampton Town, Cambridge United and Swindon Town to get some experience under his belt.

West Brom have been impressive this season but they lack depth. If Pereira got injured, WBA wouldn’t have anyone to play on the right hand side of their backline and would probably have to put Callum Styles there which wouldn’t be ideal.

Therefore, they need to consider a swoop for Furlong and it wouldn’t be a surprise to see Ipswich Town sanction a deal, either permanently or on loan. He is surplus to requirements with the Tractor Boys and should seek an exit.

#2169. Birmingham City expected to offload winger in January – Plymouth Argyle should eye move: Opinion

Plymouth Argyle are currently positioned 6th in the League One play-offs as they eye promotion to the Championship. PAFC are in the top six along with Oxford United, Luton Town and Sheffield Wednesday after 15 fixtures as the likes of Reading, Bradford City and MK Dons chasing them down.

The Pilgrims are the lowest scorers out of the top six teams in the third tier at the moment. Therefore, they should bolster their attacking department this winter with the addition of Birmingham City winger Scott Wright.

Wright, 29, is expected to be available to leave the Blues in January and he knows what it takes to get out of the third tier after helping Chris Davies’ win the title back in 2025. He has fallen out of favour with the Midlands outfit now though and a change of scene would do him good.

Plymouth Argyle missed a hatful of chances last time out as they lost 3-0 to Sheffield Wednesday at Hillsborough and they will be eager to bounce back and return to winning ways as soon as possible. They face Peterborough United at Home Park this weekend.

Plymouth Argyle could do with some more competition and depth going forward. If PAFC landed Wright, he would give them something different to pick from on the wing.

He is quick, strong and experienced so would be a handy option for the Pilgrims. The attacker could battle it out with the likes of Xavier Amaechi and Ronan Curtis for a place in Tom Cleverley’s starting XI.

Wright’s contract at Birmingham City expires in June 2027 meaning he is due to become a free agent when this campaign is over. He has seen his game time dry up with the Blues so it wouldn’t be a surprise to see them let him head out the exit door soon for a new challenge.

The ex-Scotland youth international joined them back in 2024 and has since made 24 outings, as well as spending time away on loan at Dundee. Prior to his switch, he had spells above the border at Aberdeen and Rangers.

Plymouth Argyle can offer Wright regular minutes in the division below. PAFC could provide him with a platform to show what he can do.

The Aberdeen-born man need to get out of Birmingham City for the sake of his career now. He is surplus to requirements with the Blues and will feel like he has a point to prove in the EFL.

Cleverley’s men need a response on Saturday afternoon against Peterborough United after their disappointing defeat at Sheffield Wednesday. Their upcoming opponents are in the bottom four with Doncaster Rovers, Barnsley and Bromley.

#2167. Newcastle kit fiasco sums up haphazard pre-season

Newcastle’s pre-season friendly against Bayer Leverkusen at St James’ Park took a bizarre turn when a kit clash prompted the hosts to change shirts midway through the first half.

The German team’s white away kit with blue trim proved hard to distinguish from Newcastle’s home strip, which has a white back and blue stripes across the shoulders.

During the first-half hydration break, Newcastle decided to change into their lilac third kit, which improved the viewing experience for fans who took to social media to question the original choice of kits.

Newcastle would go on to lose the game 2-1 in new manager Matthias Jaissle’s first game at St James’, a result which followed a 3-1 reverse against Everton on Wednesday evening.

Leverkusen, the 2023-24 Bundesliga champions now managed by Spaniard Carles Martínez Novell, scored after just 44 seconds through a powerful Ibrahim Maza strike.

Jaissle’s team found their feet as the first 45 minutes progressed, with Yoane Wissa their biggest attacking threat. The striker endured a difficult first season at the club, but his performances at the World Cup with DR Congo appear to have restored confidence.

Newcastle were level just before half-time, when Malick Thiaw rose highest to plant home a back-post header from a corner. Former Brentford goalkeeper Mark Flekken appealed in vain for a foul, but there was no infringement.

After changing all 10 outfield players at half-time against Everton, Jaissle was more circumspect with second-half substitutes; Rory Finneran for Lewis Miley was the only change before the 80th minute.

Newcastle faded though, and the game was won by Victor Boniface scoring just a few minutes after coming on as a substitute. The Nigeria striker wrong-footed Sean Steur with an impudent piece of skill before rolling the ball into the bottom corner.

The North-East club are coming to terms with the departures of Anthony Gordon, Sandro Tonali and Bruno Guimarães this summer, as well as manager Eddie Howe. They have one more pre-season friendly to play, against Strasbourg on Sunday, before they begin their Premier League campaign at home to Liverpool.

Newcastle United (4-2-3-1) Hornicek; M Miley (Pivas 81), Thiaw, Botman, Hall; Steur, L Miley (Finneran h-t); Elanga, Woltemade (Neave 86), Barnes; Wissa. Subs Charlton, Harris (g), Sanusi, Johnson.

Bayer Leverkusen (4-2-3-1) Flekken; Vazquez (Tape h-t), Bade, Tapsoba, Gutierrez (Belocian 70); Garcia, Fernandez (Terrier 70); Maza (Boniface 80), Tillman (Doganay 70), Moreira (Tella 80); Kofane (Schick h-t). Subs Hofmann, Arthur, Oermann, Poku, Blaswich (g), Alfa-Ruprecht, Hawighorst, Lomb (g).

Referee Michael Oliver (Durham). Attendance 39,059

A second pre-season defeat in a week for Newcastle. Just like against Everton, they faded in the second 45 minutes but Jaissle decided to leave some tired legs out there for fitness purposes. The performances of Barnes and Wissa were promising, but Newcaslte are giving up chances.

Newcastle had a free-kick with the last kick of the game, but Harvey Barnes loops the delivery straight into Flekken’s gloves.

Schik with a chance to wrap up the victory, but Hornicek saves before Thiaw clears to deny Terrier a tap in on the rebounds.

There will be just three minutes of added time to play.

Elanga fouled near the touchline, but time is running out. The free-kick won by Neave came to nothing.

Chance for Barnes! Good combination play involving Elanga and Wissa down the right, and the ball flashed across the six-yard box. Barnes almost steered it in, but did not make firm enough contact.

Newcastle attacking with more urgency since conceding, but there are moans and groans in the ground at a misplaced Elanga pass. Sean Neave has just replaced Woltemade, and the 19-year-old wins a free-kick in a crossing position with his first action of the game.

The striker has only been on the pitch a few minutes, but Boniface has restored Leverkusen’s lead. Lovely chop inside the box, which sent Steur for a hot dog, before passing the ball into the far corner. No celebration of note from a player who could be on the move this summer.

Newcastle making just their second change of the game: Pivas replaces Mason Miley at right-back.

Leverkusen have made two more changes: Moreira and Maza off, Tella and Boniface on.

The corner is straight down the throat of Flekken, and that prompsts some groans from the home fans.

Newcastle having to defend deep for long periods, but Elanga did well to win a free-kick with little support. Moreira dragged the winger down. Then Wissa’s cross is turned behind for a corner.

Woltemade’s control lets him down as he tries to bring the ball down on halfway. The game is being played at a snail’s pace at the moment, but Leverkusen are dominating the ball.

Play has now resumed. Garcia does well to defend after Lewis Hall won the ball back in a dangerous position.

Time for another hydration break and Leverkusen are making three more substitutions. We await further changes from Jaissle. Should the game finish level, there will be a penalty shoot-out to decide the winner of the Visit Malta Cup.

Rather like the mid-week friendly against Everton, Newcastle have faded after half-time. Struggling to get into the game in any meaningful way. Slick football from Leverkusen but Fernandez fails to bring the ball under his spell in the box when there was time and space to shoot.

Leverkusen just inches away from regaining the lead. It was an incisive run off the left from Afonso Moreira, who tried to place the ball into the far corner but his attempt rolled just wide of the post.

Good defensive header by Lewis Hall before Wissa wins a foul on halfway. Newcastle feeding off scraps at the moment. Elanga goes too early and is flagged offside.

Bayer Leverkusen have had the better of it in this half, but some of the shooting has been wayward. This time is it Schik’s turn to slash a shot over the bar. Fouls committed by Finneran and Botman.

Newcastle with a three-on-three on the break after Wissa does well to win the ball back on halfway, but Woltemade was too slow to get his shot away at the end of the move. More good play from Wissa.

Half a chance for Fernandez to shoot from the edge of the D, but he was leaning back and ballooned the shot miles over the bar.

Finneran, the youngster in Newcastle’s midfield, is caught on the ball but then does well to recover it. I reckon there will be a raft of changes on the hour mark.

Corner for Leverkusen after Moreira gets forward and attacks Mason Miley. The German side have additional aerial threat with Schik on the pitch. Woltemade does well to head clear.

Bayer Leverkuen kick off in the second half.

Jaissle made 10 against Everton, so wants his starters to build minutes today. Rory Finneran is coming on for Newcastle, an 18-year-old academy player. He replaces Lewis Miley in midfield. Czech striker Patrik Schik is on for Bayer Leverkusen.

The scoreline is a fair reflection of an even first half, with Wissa Newcastle’s best player. The story though, is that kit clash that prompted Newcastle to change into their lilac third kit halfway through the half.

Just the two minutes of stoppage time to play.

Newcastle equalise from a set-piece. Flekken is claiming a foul, but it is a legitimate goal. The goalkeeper was nowhere to be seen on a corner that looped all the way to be back post, and he seemed to lose his bearings allowing Thiaw to find the far corner.

Newcastle have a corner after another move in which Wissa was lively. His performances have been a ray of light in pre-season, he really struggled in his first season at the club.

Lewis Hall takes the free-kick, but he scoops it well over the bar. Elanga delivers a low cross from the right, but Garcia is well positioned to clear.

Newcastle enjoying a lot more possession now, but you cannot say they look like equalising.

That was better though, more zip in the attack with Elanga laying the ball off for Wissa and Fernandez is booked from dragging the Newcastle forward to the ground. Free-kick in a shooting position.

Good play from Lewis Hall, skipping beyond two challenges to escape from a tight spot but then his crossfield pass is intercepted. Moreira passes the ball straight out of play for a Newcastle goal kick, when there was a chance for Leverkusen to punish the hosts on the break.

Elanga tries to take on Gutierrez but his low cross is too close to Flekken. Gutierrez then buys a foul when under pressure from Lewis Miley. Game has become quite bitty following those stoppages. Maza then chops down Harvery Barnes.

The game is back under way. Changing kits has improved things visually but it is still not ideal. You wonder how on earth two clubs of this stature failed to liaise on this before the game.

Bayer Leverkusen are still on top, but play has been stopped for a hydration break. Some boos from the stands, it is not an especially hot day.

On the subject of the kit clash, Newcastle are now changing into their lilac away kit. Shades of Manchester United at the Dell all those years ago.

A short stoppage in play after Tapsoba receives a knock in the small of the back from Newcastle’s goalkeeper Hornicek. Both managers get their players over for a tactical conflab.

Leverkusen look very accomplished in possession, they are moving the ball well through the lines. Kofane was a big threat when they played Arsenal in last season’s Champions League and they are looking to find him with crosses. Mason Miley defends well at right-back, but at the expense of a corner.

Flekken called into action again, Newcastle starting to warm to their task. It was direct play from first Elanga and then Barnes, but his cross deflects towards the goalkeeper and he parries away. Then Barnes is fouled, and Newcastle have a free-kick in a crossing position near the left corner of the penalty area. Poor delivery from Barnes, Flekken claims.

Newcastle fans sing “walking in a Keegan wonderland” in unison. Bade is then penalised for catching Woltemade late as they contested an aerial challenge.

Newcastle’s best moment so far: Wissa peels off the shoulder of Bade and tested Flekken with a volley from a tight angle. The goalkeeper saved and Leverkusen scramble clear, but it was good movement from the Newcastle striker.

Leverkusen continue to make the running, enjoying more possession and most of it is coming inside Newcastle’s half. Goalkeeper Flekken takes a beat inside his own six-yard box, and his pass out is almost cut out by Woltemade.

Newcastle’s players are wearing black armbands in memory of Kevin Keegan.

There is a slightly awkward clash of kits on display: both shirts feature a lot of white, and the blue trim around the shoulders of Newcastle’s home kit is quite similar to Leverkusen’s away strip.

Kofane, who impressed up front last season in Germany, throws himself at a cross.

Fans were barely settled in their seats when Maza’s vicious left-footed strike from outside of the area flew past Hornicek. Cracking goal, not much the goalkeeper could do about it.

Matthias Jaissle walks to the home dugout for the first time as manager. Just eight days until the proper stuff begins against Liverpool, a fixture which tends to throw up entertaining games. Bayer Leverkusen keep the home players waiting in the tunnel.

The former Liverpool defender has been linked with a move to Arsenal this week. Quansah is with his team-mates but not part of the matchday squad.

Bayer Leverkusen XI: Flekken; Gutierrez, Bade, Fernandez, Moreira, Tillman, Tapsoba, Arthur, Garcia, Maza, Kofane

Subs: Blaswich, Lomb, Hofmann, Terrier, Schick, Oermann, Tape, Poku, Vazquez, Boniface, Tella, Alfa-Ruprecht, Doganay, Hawighorst

Newcastle XI: Hornicek; M Miley, Thiaw (c), Botman, Hall; Steur, L Miley; Elanga, Woltemade, Barnes; Wissa

Subs: Harris, Charlton, Pivas, Sanusi, Johnson, Finneran, Neave

Today's team to take on Bayer Leverkusen in the @VisitMalta Weekender 👊 pic.twitter.com/ZfRvucBXSq

Newcastle United continue their preparations for the new season with a home friendly against Bayer Leverkusen, hoping to improve on their pretty limp showing against Everton at Murrayfield.

New manager ​Matthias Jaissle is ‌refusing to dwell on the departure of key players that has left his club in turmoil going into the Premier League season, insisting that he sees opportunity in the situation. Jaissle was recruited from Saudi Pro League side Al-Ahli after Eddie Howe left last month, with the German coach joining a club who had lost Anthony Gordon, Sandro Tonali and captain Bruno Guimaraes in quick succession.

“It’s a challenge, a big challenge, always when there’s a transitional period. ‌But it’s ‌not time to ⁠complain, it doesn’t make sense to see the negative things,” Jaissle said.

“We want to see the chance in that situation and that’s our approach from day one. We are here to do our ⁠best job. We want to support the players, the current squad.

“There will be adjustments to see in the coming days, weeks. But for now it’s only focused on the daily work, the work we can influence. That’s all that ​matters now.”

The 38-year-old coach arrived in Newcastle ‌having won two Austrian Bundesliga titles with Red Bull Salzburg before leading Al-Ahli to back-to-back AFC Champions League crowns.

He remained tight-lipped about new signings.

“Obviously, in that transition phase, the players who left the club were ‌great ​players, great characters, experienced ⁠players. This is now a vacuum,” he said.

“Either we adjust something in the squad, by signing these players, these profiles, or other players ⁠need to step up and fill ​these positions, these leadership roles. I’m quite optimistic, we can work in both scenarios.”

Jaissle said he had a ​clear vision of how he wants his team to play.

“The phrase how Newcastle ‌should play like is front-footed, intense, ​aggressive and of course also successful. ​In the end, it’s all about what ⁠I can ⁠say and it’s also why I’m sitting here,” he said.

“The approach we have as a coaching team and the identity of Newcastle is famed for, it’s so linked so close together - which is a perfect fit in my eyes and ​that’s going to be exciting.”

#2166. Swansea City must make homegrown quota a priority to allow 30-year-old striker more game-time: Opinion

Swansea City have had an excellent start to this season. However, they have had some issues, one of which has severely hampered a summer signings ability to make his mark in South Wales.

Ross Stewart’s Swansea City career has already presented an unusual contradiction. The striker scored on his first start, helping then Vitor Matos’s side secure a 4-0 victory over Blackburn Rovers, but has since been restricted to around 171 minutes across five Championship appearances.

The Scotland international, who featured at the 2026 World Cup, arrived as a free-agent signing with a proven goalscoring record. However, his opportunities have been limited by the EFL’s homegrown rules. Scottish players do not count towards the requirement for seven qualifying players in a matchday squad.

It means a proven Championship striker is competing against administrative constraints as well as his fellow forwards. Swansea must decide whether they are overlooking a valuable attacking option or whether caution is justified given his injury record.

EFL rules require clubs to include at least seven homegrown players in their matchday squads. To qualify, players must have been registered with an English or Welsh club for three years before their 21st birthday. Stewart’s development in Scottish football means he does not meet those criteria.

Injuries to other qualifying players, alongside summer sales and loans involving academy products, have made the situation particularly challenging for the Swans.

Stewart has managed one goal from 171 minutes for Swansea this season, equivalent to approximately 0.53 goals per 90. His expected goals figure stands at 1.80, suggesting he has not yet fully capitalised on his opportunities. However, the sample size is too small to draw firm conclusions.

His aerial ability, finishing and hold-up play also offer Swansea a different attacking dimension.

There is, however, a legitimate argument for remaining cautious. Stewart’s career has been interrupted by several significant injuries, particularly during his Southampton spell, while another early-season issue has disrupted his start at Swansea.

At 30, durability is an important consideration. His goalscoring record when available is impressive, but Swansea cannot overlook the possibility that increasing his workload could create further fitness problems. Matos also has alternative attacking options.

Nevertheless, the homegrown requirement should not become an unnecessary obstacle to using one of Swansea’s most proven finishers. January provides an opportunity to address the problem by recruiting another qualifying player and giving Matos greater selection flexibility.

Stewart’s injury history makes caution understandable, but his Championship record suggests there is still considerable value in giving him a proper opportunity. Swansea should find a structural solution rather than allow their regulations to dictate how often one of their most experienced attackers can feature.

#2165. Southampton should target January move for ex-West Brom man as talk of a return is quashed: Opinion

Southampton had a mixed summer transfer window but one major blow was the sale of Taylor Harwood-Bellis. The Saints brought in multiple defenders in a bid to replace him and one of which was Zach Abbott who joined on loan.

Abbott has since struggled for gametime and fellow summer signing Keven Schlotterbeck has impressed but is now out injured. With that in mind, Tonda Eckert may may be hoping to bring in another centre-back during the January window.

Southampton are in an excellent position to push for automatic promotion this season, something that they cannot afford to take any risks with. With that in mind, they should be keeping an eye on Torbjorn Heggem’s situation.

The former West Brom man looks likely to leave Bologna this January, sparking talk of a return to the Baggies. However, while talk of a move to the Hawthorns has been quashed, a move to Southampton should be under consideration.

The 27-year-old has struggled for regular opportunities under Raffaele Palladino and has made it clear that he does not want to spend the remainder of the season sitting on the bench.

With the January transfer window approaching, the Saints could have an opportunity to bring an established international back to English football.

Heggem joined Bologna from West Brom in August 2025 after an impressive Championship campaign. He has since made 45 appearances for the Serie A side, but his involvement has decreased this season. That could leave Southampton well placed to explore a deal.

The Norwegian international has already demonstrated his ability to perform in the Championship. During the 2024/25 campaign, he made 45 league appearances for West Brom, scoring once and providing two assists. His consistency earned him both the Players’ Player of the Season and Supporters’ Player of the Season awards, underlining how highly regarded he was at The Hawthorns.

Heggem offers physical presence, aerial ability and impressive distribution. He is naturally a centre-back but can also operate at left-back, while his preference for his left foot makes him an especially useful defensive option.

His current Serie A statistics also demonstrate his technical qualities. Across seven appearances and 525 minutes this season, he has completed 93.4 per cent of his passes, recorded 43 line-breaking passes and made 27 clearances. He has also won 12 aerial duels and earned 25 caps for Norway.

Southampton would face competition for his signature, and his wages could prove a stumbling block. Nevertheless, he is approaching his prime, has proven himself in the Championship and possesses experience at a higher level.

With promotion ambitions potentially shaping their January business, Southampton should seriously consider swooping for Heggem. He could be an outstanding addition to their defensive options.

#2164. £1.2m Blackburn Rovers man has a chance to lock down a starting spot, beginning with crucial Burnley tie: Opinion

Blackburn Rovers face Burnley in a Lancashire derby on Sunday. It’s a big occasion at Ewood Park, not only because of the rivalry, but because it marks Rovers’ first game since Tony Mowbray stepped back as head coach due to health issues earlier this week.

Damien Johnson and Mike Dodds will lead the side on an interim basis, but the temporary bosses will not be able to call upon star right-back Ryan Alebiosu. The Nigerian international has undergone surgery on his knee and is set to be sidelined until the New Year.

Summer signing Gady Beyuku replaced Alebiosu when he hobbled off against Stoke City. And, he played the full 90 minutes against West Ham while the medical staff waited for a verdict on Alebiosu’s blow.

Now that we know how long Blackburn Rovers’ go-to right-back will be out for, Beyuku has a real chance to make the starting role his own.

Most clubs have at least two players to cover in every position, and that is the case at Blackburn Rovers. With Alebiosu out, Beyuku is the only natural senior right-back available for selection, paving the way for him to come into the side.

A reported fee of around £1.2m was paid to sign the 20-year-old from Modena in the summer, so there is clear belief in his potential. It was widely expected that Beyuku would be cover and competition for Alebiosu, but the injury thrusts him into the limelight.

He’s shown signs of growing into Championship football early on as well. His second-half against West Ham was the best we’ve seen from Beyuku to date, offering a lively presence up and down the right-hand side.

The Frenchman saw to his duties at the back with 10 defensive contributions, but he was forward-thinking and frequently involved in possession like Alebiosu as well. Beyuku completed 26 of his 29 passes, nine of which were line-breaking.

He was dispossessed twice, but this does come with the territory of being a progressive right-back who is still developing and settling into a new league (all stats via FotMob).

Alebiosu’s shoes are big ones to fill. It might not have been planned that Beyuku would assume a starting role this early into his Blackburn Rovers, given his age and need to grow into Championship football.

It means that, despite the fact he needs to deliver, there should be an element of patience.

If Johnson and Dodds feel Beyuku needs to be taken out of the side, there are options to consider. Kristi Montgomery has a key role in midfield alongside Sam Morsy but he has covered at right-back before. Matty Litherland is working his way back from injury too, while Lewis Miller is a longer-term absentee.

Beyuku will have the firm backing of the Rovers faithful. A strong display against arch-rivals Burnley this Sunday would be a sure-fire way for the youngster to endear himself to the fans and announce himself in Championship football.

#2163. Rashford returns and Fernandes misses another penalty as Man Utd humbled by Amorim

Bruno Fernandes missed a penalty for the second successive pre-season friendly as Manchester United were beaten 4-2 by former head coach Ruben Amorim’s AC Milan.

Fernandes, who rolled a spot-kick wide in a shoot-out against Leeds in midweek, was gifted the chance to open his pre-season account in Wroclaw, Poland.

His effort was on target this time, but Milan keeper Lorenzo Torriani dived to his right to beat the shot away.

Fernandes did at least get an assist, taking the second-minute corner which was powerfully headed home by Harry Maguire to give United the lead.

Amorim’s Milan equalised after a defensive mix-up allowed Gonçalo Ramos to break into the area and square for Samuel Chukwueze to roll home.

United were awarded their penalty just before half-time after Koni De Winter tugged back Youri Tielemans, but Torriani guessed correctly to leave Fernandes frustrated.

United retook the lead after Patrick Dorgu pounced on a loose backpass from Filippo Terracciano, but Milan equalised again when Chukwueze’s wayward shot was poked in by Alphadjo Cissè at the far post.

Marcus Rashford, who was frozen out by Amorim and loaned out to Aston Villa and then Barcelona, came on as a substitute on the hour for his first United appearance since returning from Spain.

But Milan went ahead in the 68th minute when Chukwueze’s cross was headed home by Ramos, and three minutes later former Chelsea midfielder Ruben Loftus-Cheek raced through to hit the fourth and cap a performance that underlines United’s need for reinforcements before their season starts in earnest next Saturday, away at Hull City.

Manchester United (4-2-3-1) Lammens; Mazraoui (Dalot 61), Maguire, Heaven (Martinez 61), Shaw; Tielemans, Santos (Mainoo 61); Diallo (Lacey 61), Fernandes, Dorgu (Rashford 61); Cunha (Mbeumo 61). Subs Zirkzee, Yoro, J Fletcher, T Fletcher, Amass, Collyer, Mee (g), Heath (g), Gabriel. Booked Dorgu.

#2162. Derby County & West Ham must provide Southampton escape route for Man City star

Southampton have fought their way back up the Championship table and into play-off contention despite a four-point deduction at the start of the season, as head coach Tonda Eckert may already have one eye on the upcoming January transfer window.

In what is now old news, Southampton were deducted four points at the start of the Championship campaign after being found guilty of spying on opponents last season, and were also thrown out of the play-offs as a result.

This time around, the Saints are looking to claw their way into the top eight legitimately with Eckert still at the helm, and following eight wins, four draws, and three defeats after 15 league games, they are well within play-off contention.

The German boss has stamped his authority at St Mary's following the summer transfer window, though not every addition has necessarily worked out the way he would've hoped.

Football League World has asked AI to predict the 2027/28 EFL Championship table if Manchester United and Manchester City were in it

Southampton saw the likes of Shea Charles, Taylor Harwood-Bellis, Cameron Archer, and Ben Brereton Diaz head out of the St Mary's exit door over the summer, as the likes of Lewis Dobbin, James Ward-Prowse, Keven Schlotterbeck, and Divin Mubama joined in the opposite direction.

Mubama, 21, joined on loan from Manchester City after spending the previous campaign on loan with Stoke City in the Championship, with the England youth international expecting to push Canadian striker Cyle Larin hard for a place in the starting XI.

Divin Mubama Stoke City Championship statistics 2025/26 (Data as per FotMob)

However, despite his 13 league appearances thus far, only one of those has come as a start, with Mubama being limited to just over 200 minutes of league football so far this campaign, struggling to make an impact in those limited minutes.

Naturally, his parent club, Man City, won't be pleased by his lack of game time and subsequent development, as Football League World has taken a look at two Championship clubs that should consider rescuing the 21-year-old this winter.

Derby County find themselves in the midst of a scrap towards the foot of the Championship table as things stand, with two wins, four draws, and nine defeats in their opening 15 league games piling pressure on head coach John Eustace.

A 3-1 home victory over Bolton Wanderers just before the international break has seemingly given Eustace a lifeline at Pride Park, and if his side can start to pick up some form, he may well start turning his attention to the January transfer window sooner rather than later.

Up top, Derby can boast the likes of Carlton Morris, Henrik Meister, and Lars-Jorgen Salvesen, whilst both Patrick Agyemang, Mohammed Fuseini, and Rhian Brewster can also operate in that position; on the face of it, a new centre-forward option is not needed.

However, those six options have just five goals between them at this stage in the season, and whilst numerous factors can be attributed to this, Derby simply cannot afford to settle with what they have got.

The Rams, according to Pete O'Rourke via X, were closing in on a move for Mubama earlier on in the summer window, but for one reason or another, the deal never went through, and the England youth international headed to Southampton instead.

The sort of centre-forward that Eustace loves: quick, strong, capable of holding up the ball and interlinking play as well as getting in behind, and with smart movement, Mubama would be an excellent addition for Derby, and if he can be provided with chances regularly, he will help the Rams look towards the play-offs instead of the relegation zone.

Would you like to see Divin Mubama at your club? Do you agree with our picks? Where do you think he should go? Share your thoughts below!

Mubama spent the bulk of his youth playing days in the West Ham United academy, going on to make 18 first-team appearances for the Hammers and scoring once before heading to Man City in the summer of 2024 for just £1.2 million compensation.

West Ham have endured a tricky start to life back in the Championship, with Nuno Espírito Santo being handed his marching orders after 13 games and being replaced by former Fulham, Bournemouth, and Burnley boss Scott Parker.

Parker made over a century of appearances for the East London outfit during his playing days, and it is hoped that he can steer the club in the right direction following his appointment.

The former England midfielder may well already have one eye on the January transfer window, with Alan Nixon having recently reported via Patreon that West Ham are targeting a new striker and left-back this winter.

Taty Castellanos and Pablo Felipe have not handled the drop into the second tier especially well, as Joel Piroe has emerged as the preferred option up front as things stand, though the Dutchman could do with some competition.

As a cheap loan, and a player who understands exactly what the West Ham DNA is, Mubama would be a solid fit for the Hammers and would offer a completely different profile to Piroe.

If Castellanos and Pablo leave, a new striker will be needed regardless, and following his lack of minutes with Southampton, Parker has the opportunity to show the Saints exactly what they've been missing out on with a January swoop.

#2161. Roberto Di Matteo lifts lid on West Brom decision that shocked him

Roberto Di Matteo has admitted that he was ‘shocked’ West Brom sacked him back in 2011 as they battled to stay in the Premier League.

The Italian had a successful playing career, and he turned to management after retiring, initially starting out with MK Dons.

A move to Albion would follow, and Di Matteo would also work for Chelsea, Schalke and Aston Villa, with the high point undoubtedly coming with the Blues, who he won the Champions League and FA Cup with as interim boss.

Whilst nothing will surpass winning those trophies with Chelsea, Di Matteo really made his name as a manager during his time at The Hawthorns with West Brom.

After making the step up from League One outfit MK Dons, Di Matteo built an Albion side that won promotion to the Premier League in his first season at the club.

They did so by playing a brand of football that the fans really enjoyed, and they carried that into the top flight. Whilst an opening day 6-0 defeat at Chelsea was concerning, the players quickly adapted to the Premier League.

The Baggies had picked up 15 points from their first nine games, which included a memorable 3-2 win at Arsenal, a 2-2 draw at Old Trafford, and they also picked up a point from Tottenham.

So, Albion were performing very well, and they continued the entertaining style of play that had made them such a good watch the season before.

However, that form didn’t continue, and Di Matteo was ultimately dismissed in February, after a miserable period of seven defeats in nine.

And, speaking to Mick McCarthy and Tony Pulis on The Managers podcast, Di Matteo couldn’t hide how disappointed he was at the decision, with Albion outside the relegation zone at the time.

“I was a bit surprised and shocked. We’ve all worked in smaller clubs, so when you have injuries to one or two key players, it’s difficult. Up until the Christmas period we were looking really good, but Jonas Olsson, the centre-back, got injured for a while, and one or two others were not available.

“In the Christmas period, you play every two or three days and you don’t have a deep squad, so you struggle a bit. But, again, we were coming out of it, and I was really shocked.”

You can understand why Di Matteo was surprised at the decision, as he will have been tasked with keeping the team in the league, and he will argue he was on track to do that.

FLW ranks the eight most famous footballers to have played in the Championship at some point in their career.

Plus, this was the first tough spell he had in charge of Albion, so he would’ve hoped that he had enough credit in the bank to be given an opportunity to turn things around.

Yet, football is a ruthless business, and the Albion board needed to make what they felt was the best decision for the club, and the reality is that, in hindsight, it was a call that paid off.

They had been on a terrible run over a period of a few months, and they would say the trend was pointing towards relegation.

West Brom fans, how do you reflect on Roberto Di Matteo's time in charge? Let us know in the comments below.

As it turns out, West Brom appointed Roy Hodgson, in what was a masterstroke. He kept the team up with ease, and they had another seven consecutive years as a Premier League before relegation in 2018.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

The Chairman of the Board was Jeremy Peace, who always had his own interests at heart.

Di Matteo was doing well, but with one hand tied behind his back for strengthening the squad.

His premature sacking was the start of a difficult period with Peace at the helm.

The £3.5m debt that Peace created by 'loaning' the money to himself has just been paid off by the current owners. The previous owners to whom Peace shad old the club (down the river) took his example and loaned themselves £5m during covid.

Curiously, I can find little information on the web about Mr Peace's current whereabouts, but I doubt he has a season ticker for the Hawthorns.
